This low energy and loss of interest only started after I began transitioning into retirement, and it's the first time I've dealt with something like this for such a long period.\n\nDoctor: Along with the low energy and loss of interest, have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day? Or is it more that you feel physically exhausted without feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: It is definitely both for me. While I feel physically exhausted, like my internal battery is only at half-power, there is also this low, gray cloud hanging over me most days. It makes me feel like nothing is worth getting excited about, and I catch myself asking \"what's the point?\" even when doing things I used to love.\n\nDoctor: Do these symptoms feel mainly tied to the stress of retiring, so that if you were more settled in that transition you think they would ease? Or do they feel like a depression that has taken on a life of its own beyond the retirement change?\n\nPatient: It definitely feels like it has taken on a life of its own at this point. Even though I've been retired for over three years now and should be settled, this low, gray cloud has just stuck with me constantly. It affects everything, even when I'm out fishing or sitting with my grandkids, so it feels like much more than just transitional stress.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself? If yes, have you had any plan or intent to act on them?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any thoughts of hurting myself or any plans to do that. While I do get down sometimes and wonder what the point of my daily activities is, I have never wanted to harm myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any symptoms like hearing or seeing things others don’t, or fixed beliefs that others say aren’t true? Also, are you still able to manage basic daily tasks like bathing, dressing, and eating?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never experienced anything like hearing or seeing things, or having unusual beliefs. I can still handle basic grooming and dressing, but my eating has become very inconsistent, and I've been letting chores and appointments slide to the point where my wife has to pick up the slack.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been during this time—trouble falling asleep, waking during the night, waking too early, or sleeping much more than usual? Has this been happening most days?\n\nPatient: I go to bed around ten, but I'll lie awake for an hour or more with my mind racing about wasting my retirement and falling behind in technology. Then, I wake up around four or five in the morning and just stare at the ceiling, unable to fall back asleep. This happens most nights and leaves me feeling completely worn out."

I can still handle basic grooming and dressing, but my eating has become very inconsistent, and I've been letting chores and appointments slide to the point where my wife has to pick up the slack.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been during this time—trouble falling asleep, waking during the night, waking too early, or sleeping much more than usual? Has this been happening most days?\n\nPatient: I go to bed around ten, but I'll lie awake for an hour or more with my mind racing about wasting my retirement and falling behind in technology.
